### Learning Objective

Explain how information is measured in bits and bytes.

### Why This Topic Exists

A bit is a binary digit: 0 or 1. A byte is eight bits and can represent 256 distinct patterns. Text, images, and instructions are all stored as bits; the meaning comes from the encoding we agree on. CS50 Week 0 is the selected source for this representation idea. Do not complete

## Hexadecimal

### Metadata

- Slug: `cf-hexadecimal`
- Topic ID: 3
- Domain: CS Foundations
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: WORKING_KNOWLEDGE
- Sequential order: 3
- Readiness: **READY**

### Learning Objective

Use hexadecimal as a compact view of binary.

### Why This Topic Exists

Hexadecimal is base-16. Each hex digit is exactly four bits, so 1111 is F and a byte is two hex digits. CS50 Week 0 teaches binary/decimal; hex is the same positional idea with grouping. You will see hex in memory dumps, color codes (#RRGGBB), and addresses.

### Learning Objective

Explain registers as the CPU's fastest working storage.

## RAM

### Metadata

- Slug: `cf-ram`
- Topic ID: 7
- Domain: CS Foundations
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: WORKING_KNOWLEDGE
- Sequential order: 7
- Readiness: **READY**
- Spine: yes

### Learning Objective

Explain RAM as volatile working memory.

### Why This Topic Exists

RAM (main memory) holds the running program's code and data. It is volatile: contents disappear when power is gone. It is larger and slower than registers, smaller and faster than disk. A program on disk is not executing until loaded.

## Cache

### Metadata

- Slug: `cf-cache`
- Topic ID: 8
- Domain: CS Foundations
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: WORKING_KNOWLEDGE
- Sequential order: 8
- Readiness: **READY**

### Learning Objective

Explain why caches exist between CPU and RAM.

### Why This Topic Exists

A cache is a small fast memory that stores recently used data and instructions so the CPU does not wait on RAM every time. It works because programs reuse nearby data (locality). You do not need cache-coherence protocols at this level.

### Learning Objective

Describe fetch-decode-execute at a conceptual level.

### Why This Topic Exists

The processor repeats: fetch the next instruction from memory, decode what it means, execute it (often with the ALU), then continue. A simple assignment becomes several instructions. CS50 Week 1 shows source becoming machine code; it does not walk microarchitecture pipelines.

- Topic ID: 68
- Domain: Java
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 68
- Readiness: **READY**
- Spine: yes

### Learning Objective

Declare, initialize, and choose Java primitive types; distinguish them from String and other references.

### Why This Topic Exists

Java primitives are boolean, byte, short, int, long, float, double, and char. They are not objects. String is a reference type (a class), even though it has literals. `var` infers a type from the initializer; it is not a dynamic type. Uninitialized local variables cannot be read.

### Learning Objective

### Why This Topic Exists

Widening conversions (int → long → float → double) can be implicit. Narrowing (double → int, long → int) needs a cast and can truncate. Integer overflow wraps in two's complement; it does not throw. Mixing int division (`5 / 2`) truncates. Boxing (int ↔ Integer) is separate from

## break and continue

### Metadata

- Slug: `java-break-continue`
- Topic ID: 75
- Domain: Java
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 75
- Readiness: **NEEDS_REVIEW**
- Spine: yes

### Learning Objective

Use break and continue deliberately; avoid using them to hide messy structure.

### Why This Topic Exists

break leaves the innermost loop or switch. continue skips the rest of this iteration. Labeled break exists but is rarely the clearest design. Prefer a boolean condition when it reads better.

### Prerequisites

- **REQUIRED**: `java-loops` — Loops (Required gate for java-break-continue)

## Method basics

### Metadata

- Slug: `java-method-basics`
- Topic ID: 76
- Domain: Java
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 76
- Readiness: **READY**
- Spine: yes

### Learning Objective

Declare methods with parameters and return values; explain Java pass-by-value; refactor a monolithic main.

### Why This Topic Exists

A method has a name, parameter list, return type (or void), and body. Callers pass arguments. Java is pass-by-value: primitives are copied; for objects, the reference value is copied, so the callee can mutate the same object but cannot make the caller's variable point elsewhere.

### Learning Objective

### Why This Topic Exists

Overloading: same name, different parameter types or arity. The compiler picks the best match. You cannot overload on return type only. Constructors can overload too (next OOP module). Do not confuse with overriding (runtime, inheritance).

## Scope

- Slug: `java-scope`
- Topic ID: 78
- Domain: Java
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 78
- Readiness: **READY**
- Spine: yes

### Learning Objective

Reason about local vs parameter vs class-level variables and shadowing.

### Why This Topic Exists

A local variable lives from its declaration to the end of its block. Parameters are locals. Fields (later) live as long as the object. Nested blocks can shadow. Loops' for-init variables are scoped to the loop.

- Domain: Java
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 81
- Readiness: **READY**

### Learning Objective

Build strings efficiently with StringBuilder and know when String is enough.

### Why This Topic Exists

StringBuilder is a mutable sequence. append/insert/delete mutate the same object. It is not thread-safe (StringBuffer is the older synchronized cousin; you do not need it here). Use String for immutable values and APIs; StringBuilder for loops that assemble text.

### Learning Objective

Hide representation, protect invariants, and use accessors only when they earn their keep.

### Why This Topic Exists

Encapsulation: keep fields private and expose a small API that preserves invariants (balance >= 0). Getters/setters are not mandatory boilerplate. A public setter that allows any value destroys the invariant. Prefer immutable fields when mutation is not part of the domain.

- Topic ID: 91

- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 91
- Readiness: **READY**

### Learning Objective

Handle recoverable failures with try/catch/finally without swallowing everything.

### Why This Topic Exists

Exceptions signal that a method cannot complete normally. try/catch handles a specific type you can recover from. finally runs for cleanup. Prefer try-with-resources for Closeable I/O (File I/O module). Do not catch Exception everywhere. Empty catch blocks hide bugs.

## Generic types

### Metadata

- Slug: `java-generic-types`
- Topic ID: 99
- Domain: Java
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 99
- Readiness: **READY**
- Spine: yes

### Learning Objective

Declare generic classes and methods for type-safe containers.

### Why This Topic Exists

Generics parameterize types: Box<T>, List<String>. They exist for compile-time type safety. At runtime, type erasure means the JVM sees raw types (conceptual level only). Do not overteach PECS yet — next topic. Helsinki Part 12 type parameters is the exercise layer.

### Learning Objective

Start work on another thread (Runnable/Executor) and contrast process vs thread.

### Why This Topic Exists

A process is an OS program instance. A thread is a unit of execution inside a process sharing memory. Runnable is the task; Thread/Executor runs it. ExecutorService is the modern default over raw new Thread. Virtual threads (Dev.java) are a modern scaling feature — reference only

- Topic ID: 111

- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 111

### Learning Objective

Demonstrate a race and fix it with synchronized/locking; explain visibility at a high level.

### Why This Topic Exists

A race: two threads update shared mutable state with no happens-before. synchronized / locks make a critical section. Visibility: without synchronization, another thread may never see your write. Concurrent collections exist; don't roll a concurrent HashMap by hand. Not a DSA alg

javac emits bytecode in .class files. The JVM loads classes and executes bytecode (then JIT may compile hot methods to native). javap -c disassembles. You do not need to memorize opcodes.

## Garbage collection intro

### Metadata

- Topic ID: 114
- Domain: Java
- Lane/Track: CORE (CORE)
- Depth: STRONG
- Sequential order: 114
- Readiness: **READY**
- Spine: yes

### Learning Objective

Explain reachability and that GC reclaims unreachable objects.

### Why This Topic Exists

Garbage collection frees heap objects that are not reachable from GC roots (stacks, statics, etc.). You don't call free(). Memory leaks in Java are usually accidental retained references. Tuning collectors is out of scope. Profiling awareness: jcmd/JFR exist; you don't need them
